It usually feels clear to me at first. Like I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and in my head it seems connected. But then when I’m talking, the words start jumping around—like I’ll go from the delivery trucks to Thursday, then something about the church grill, then my boss’s tone. I don’t always catch it right away, but people start looking at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF25.9, F31.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m having trouble getting my words out in a way people can follow. Like at work, when I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, I’ll start on one thing and then my words jump around—from trucks, to Thursdays, to the church grill, to my boss’s tone. I know it sounds mixed up, and people just look at me like they don’t understand what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how your thoughts and words jump around? Do you feel like your thoughts are connected or do they feel completely unrelated when you're speaking?\n\nPatient: It feels connected to me at first, like I know what I’m trying to say. But when I start talking, it comes out all mixed up. I’ll be trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then I’m suddenly talking about the trucks, or Thursday, or the church grill, or the way my boss sounded. In my head there’s some link, but other people don’t seem to follow it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t hear or see?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t noticed anything like seeing things that aren’t there. And I don’t really hear voices talking to me. Mostly it’s just hard to get my thoughts out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that other people say are strange or not based in reality, even if they make sense to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think so, not in a big way. People mostly tell me I’m hard to follow, not that what I’m saying is unreal. I mean, what I’m thinking usually makes sense to me, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that you know other people might find odd or hard to believe, even if they make perfect sense to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I probably have opinions people don’t agree with, but not things I’d call hard to believe. If something sounds odd, it’s usually more that I’m not explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or experiences that you know are different from what most people experience, even if they make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t think I have experiences like that. Sometimes I guess I get confused or people don’t understand what I’m trying to say, but I haven’t noticed anything that feels different from what other people experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed something strongly that turned out to be completely different from what others believed or what was actually happening, even if it made sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: I can’t really think of a time like that. I mean, I’ve been wrong about things before, like anybody, but not where I strongly believed something and everyone else said it wasn’t happening. At least nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you had to keep talking, that your thoughts were coming so fast you couldn't keep up, and others told you that you were talking too much or too fast?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m trying to explain something, but I wouldn’t say my thoughts are racing or that I can’t keep up with them. I don’t remember people telling me I was talking too fast like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, to the point it affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have bad days where I feel tired or kind of discouraged, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless or stopped caring about things to the point I couldn’t get through my day. Nothing like that really stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly that others said was not true, even if it made perfect sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ve had opinions or guesses that turned out wrong, sure, but I can’t think of a time where I was convinced of something and other people were telling me it wasn’t real or wasn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you had to keep talking, even if you weren’t sure what you were saying, because you felt pressured or anxious if you stopped?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s happened. I might keep talking if I’m nervous or trying to explain myself, but not like I feel pressured and can’t stop, or like I don’t know what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were going faster than usual, to the point it affected your behavior or decisions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep great, but not where I felt unusually energized or like my thoughts were racing and it made me do things differently. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens just before your speech becomes mixed up? Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disconnected in your mind, or do they still feel logical and clear to you even if others don’t follow them?\n\nPatient: It usually feels clear to me at first. Like I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and in my head it seems connected. But then when I’m talking, the words start jumping around—like I’ll go from the delivery trucks to Thursday, then something about the church grill, then my boss’s tone. I don’t always catch it right away, but people start looking at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, what I’m thinking usually makes sense to me, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that you know other people might find odd or hard to believe, even if they make perfect sense to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I probably have opinions people don’t agree with, but not things I’d call hard to believe. If something sounds odd, it’s usually more that I’m not explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or experiences that you know are different from what most people experience, even if they make sense to you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t think I have experiences like that. Sometimes I guess I get confused or people don’t understand what I’m trying to say, but I haven’t noticed anything that feels different from what other people experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed something strongly that turned out to be completely different from what others believed or what was actually happening, even if it made sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: I can’t really think of a time like that. I mean, I’ve been wrong about things before, like anybody, but not where I strongly believed something and everyone else said it wasn’t happening. At least nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you had to keep talking, that your thoughts were coming so fast you couldn't keep up, and others told you that you were talking too much or too fast?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m trying to explain something, but I wouldn’t say my thoughts are racing or that I can’t keep up with them. I don’t remember people telling me I was talking too fast like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, to the point it affected your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have bad days where I feel tired or kind of discouraged, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless or stopped caring about things to the point I couldn’t get through my day. Nothing like that really stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly that others said was not true, even if it made perfect sense to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ve had opinions or guesses that turned out wrong, sure, but I can’t think of a time where I was convinced of something and other people were telling me it wasn’t real or wasn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you had to keep talking, even if you weren’t sure what you were saying, because you felt pressured or anxious if you stopped?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s happened. I might keep talking if I’m nervous or trying to explain myself, but not like I feel pressured and can’t stop, or like I don’t know what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were going faster than usual, to the point it affected your behavior or decisions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep great, but not where I felt unusually energized or like my thoughts were racing and it made me do things differently. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens just before your speech becomes mixed up? Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disconnected in your mind, or do they still feel logical and clear to you even if others don’t follow them?\n\nPatient: It usually feels clear to me at first. Like I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and in my head it seems connected. But then when I’m talking, the words start jumping around—like I’ll go from the delivery trucks to Thursday, then something about the church grill, then my boss’s tone. I don’t always catch it right away, but people start looking at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like your speech sped up because your thoughts were coming so fast, even if you weren’t trying to rush?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t really notice my thoughts coming fast like that. It’s more that I’m trying to explain something, and then what comes out doesn’t line up right. I’m not usually trying to rush, but I don’t feel like my mind is speeding up either.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your thoughts felt disorganized or jumbled inside your head even when you weren't trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it feels a little jumbled even before I say it out loud. Like I’ll be thinking about the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind kind of skips over to the delivery trucks, or Thursday, or even something like the church grill or how my boss said something. It makes sense to me in the moment, but it doesn’t stay in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even though there was no evidence to support it and other people strongly disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I can’t really think of a time like that. I mean, I’ve been sure about things and other people didn’t agree with me, but I don’t know if I’d say there was no evidence. I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t, and other people tried to tell you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t think of a clear time where everybody was showing me it wasn’t real and I still knew it was true. I’ve had disagreements, sure, but I don’t know that I’d call it that. Maybe I’d need an example of what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I might keep talking if I’m nervous or trying to explain myself, but not like I feel pressured and can’t stop, or like I don’t know what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were going faster than usual, to the point it affected your behavior or decisions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep great, but not where I felt unusually energized or like my thoughts were racing and it made me do things differently. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens just before your speech becomes mixed up? Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disconnected in your mind, or do they still feel logical and clear to you even if others don’t follow them?\n\nPatient: It usually feels clear to me at first. Like I’m trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and in my head it seems connected. But then when I’m talking, the words start jumping around—like I’ll go from the delivery trucks to Thursday, then something about the church grill, then my boss’s tone. I don’t always catch it right away, but people start looking at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like your speech sped up because your thoughts were coming so fast, even if you weren’t trying to rush?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t really notice my thoughts coming fast like that. It’s more that I’m trying to explain something, and then what comes out doesn’t line up right. I’m not usually trying to rush, but I don’t feel like my mind is speeding up either.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your thoughts felt disorganized or jumbled inside your head even when you weren't trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it feels a little jumbled even before I say it out loud. Like I’ll be thinking about the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind kind of skips over to the delivery trucks, or Thursday, or even something like the church grill or how my boss said something. It makes sense to me in the moment, but it doesn’t stay in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even though there was no evidence to support it and other people strongly disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I can’t really think of a time like that. I mean, I’ve been sure about things and other people didn’t agree with me, but I don’t know if I’d say there was no evidence. I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t, and other people tried to tell you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t think of a clear time where everybody was showing me it wasn’t real and I still knew it was true. I’ve had disagreements, sure, but I don’t know that I’d call it that. Maybe I’d need an example of what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly, even when there was clear evidence it wasn't true, and other people tried to convince you otherwise but you still knew in your mind that it was real?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been stubborn about things before, like anyone, but not where people were showing me clear proof and I still just knew something unreal was real. Nothing comes to mind like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t, and other people tried to tell you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Nothing comes to mind like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn't, and other people tried to convince you otherwise but you still knew in your mind that it was real?\n\nPatient: No, I really can’t think of a time like that. I don’t really notice my thoughts coming fast like that. It’s more that I’m trying to explain something, and then what comes out doesn’t line up right. I’m not usually trying to rush, but I don’t feel like my mind is speeding up either.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your thoughts felt disorganized or jumbled inside your head even when you weren't trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it feels a little jumbled even before I say it out loud. Like I’ll be thinking about the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind kind of skips over to the delivery trucks, or Thursday, or even something like the church grill or how my boss said something. It makes sense to me in the moment, but it doesn’t stay in a straight line.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even though there was no evidence to support it and other people strongly disagreed with you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I can’t really think of a time like that. I mean, I’ve been sure about things and other people didn’t agree with me, but I don’t know if I’d say there was no evidence. I’d have to think about what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t, and other people tried to tell you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t think of a clear time where everybody was showing me it wasn’t real and I still knew it was true. I’ve had disagreements, sure, but I don’t know that I’d call it that. Maybe I’d need an example of what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly, even when there was clear evidence it wasn't true, and other people tried to convince you otherwise but you still knew in your mind that it was real?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been stubborn about things before, like anyone, but not where people were showing me clear proof and I still just knew something unreal was real. Nothing comes to mind like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn't, and other people tried to convince you otherwise but you still knew in your mind that it was real?\n\nPatient: No, I really can’t think of a time like that. I’ve had strong opinions before, but if someone had clear proof I was wrong, I don’t think I’d keep insisting it was real. Nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was definitely true, even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t, and other people tried to convince you otherwise but you still knew in your mind that it was real?\n\nPatient: I don’t have an example of that. I know we’ve kind of gone over it, but no—if there was clear evidence and people were trying to show me, I don’t think I’d just keep believing the opposite. Nothing like that really stands out to me."
